# Story 4.2: Rehydrate create dialogue from persisted workflow state on restart

Status: ready-for-dev

<!-- Note: Validation is optional. Run validate-create-story for quality check before dev-story. -->

## Story

As a platform engineer,
I want active create dialogue actors to be reconstructible from workflow state,
so that workflow continuity survives process restarts or actor loss.

## Acceptance Criteria

1. Given the process restarts during an active create flow, when root reloads active workflow records, then it can recreate the `createDialogueMachine(flowId)` child from persisted workflow artifacts.
2. Given child-local memory is lost, when the child is recreated, then workflow truth remains intact because root owned the authoritative state.

## Sequencing / Dependencies

- Target confirmed: Epic 4, Story 4.2.
- Required predecessors: Epic 1 stories 1.1–1.3, Epic 2 stories 2.1–2.3, Epic 3 stories 3.1–3.3, and Story 4.1, because restart rehydration depends on durable workflow state, stable `flowId`, baseline/versioning, supervised dialogue routing, lookup and adjudication contracts, plus distinct lifecycle phase modeling.
- This story is a prerequisite for Story 4.3 terminal live-actor shutdown coordination.
- If repo behavior conflicts with older assumptions, prefer `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md`.

## Tasks / Subtasks

- [x] Add restart-ready rehydration input assembly in root for active create workflows (AC: 1)
  - [x] On root startup/reload, inspect persisted active workflow records and identify non-terminal create flows that should have a live create-dialogue child.
  - [x] Reconstruct create-dialogue start/resume input from persisted workflow artifacts rather than child-local memory.
  - [x] Preserve the same `flowId` and lifecycle phase when rebuilding the child.

- [x] Recreate the flow-keyed `createDialogueMachine(flowId)` child from persisted workflow state (AC: 1)
  - [x] Use the response supervisor path introduced in Epic 2 to recreate the create-dialogue child for each eligible active flow.
  - [x] Provide the child with persisted workflow artifacts needed for continuity, including `createPlan`, authoritative `createDrafts`, `responseSpec`, feedback, revision/baseline metadata, and read-only `campaignEntityIndex` where applicable.
  - [x] Keep the recreated child keyed to the same `flowId` used by root and create.

- [x] Treat child-local memory as disposable and reconstructible (AC: 2)
  - [x] Do not rely on transient prompt assembly state, scratch retrieval state, or other child-local memory as the only source of workflow truth.
  - [x] Ensure the rehydrated child can continue from root-owned persisted artifacts even if all local child memory was lost.
  - [x] Document clearly in code/comments which child-local fields are disposable versus which artifacts must come from root persistence.

- [x] Keep create actor recreation lazy where architecture says it should be lazy (AC: 1, 2)
  - [x] Recreate or awaken the create-dialogue child for active flows on restart.
  - [x] Keep `createmachine` lazy or on-demand for adjudication/persistence rather than forcing all create actors to be eagerly live at restart if architecture does not require it.
  - [x] Preserve the root/create/response ownership boundaries during restart.

- [x] Keep Story 4.2 tightly scoped to restart/rehydration only (AC: 1, 2)
  - [x] Do not implement terminal live-actor shutdown here; that belongs to Story 4.3.
  - [x] Do not redesign lifecycle phase semantics already addressed in Story 4.1.
  - [x] Do not add UI/session rendering work.

- [x] Remediate restart hydration to recreate every eligible non-terminal create flow on root reload (AC: 1)
  - [x] Iterate persisted restartable create records and recreate each eligible `createDialogueMachine(flowId)` child instead of selecting only one candidate.
  - [x] Canonicalize hydrated flow identifiers before matching them back to persisted active flow records.
- [x] Remediate rehydration payloads so persisted lifecycle phase survives restart (AC: 1, 2)
  - [x] Thread `snapshotRef.phase` through `WorkflowCreateDialogueResumeInput`, `ResponseSupervisorInput`, and `CreateDialogueMachineInput`.
  - [x] Preserve `dialogue_collecting`, `review_ready`, and `save_pending` when rebuilding the child from root-owned state.
- [x] Remediate root reload behavior so live create dialogue is recreated during workflow hydration, not on the next turn (AC: 1)
  - [x] Move child reconstruction into the workflow reload path that seeds `activeFlows`.
  - [x] Keep the flow-first turn path as the runtime ingress, but do not rely on it as the first point of child recreation.
  - [x] Exclude terminal create records from restart hydration eligibility, or gate hydration on a non-terminal workflow-status check before replaying a record.
  - [x] Persist and consume the hydrated create lifecycle phase inside `create-dialogue-machine.ts` so restart rehydration can preserve distinct phase behavior.
  - [x] Attach restart hydration results to root-owned state, or otherwise retain the recreated child actors instead of dropping the transient response supervisor output.
  - [x] Add collision-safe handling when canonicalizing hydrated active flow IDs so duplicate trimmed IDs cannot silently overwrite earlier records.

- [x] Review follow-up: finish the remaining restart-hydration fixes called out by the latest review.
  - [x] Keep canonicalized hydrated flow IDs aligned with every dependent reference, including `focusedFlowId`, `recentFlowIds`, and nested `responseBaseline.flowId` values.
  - [x] Preserve the hydrated create phase through the `responsemachine` supervisor boundary and root restart bookkeeping so `currentMachineStateValue` survives rehydration.


- [x] Merge the child supervisor’s restart patch into the root hydration patch so `focusedFlowId` and `currentTurn.currentMachineStateValue` survive restart.
  - [x] Forward or merge `snapshot.output.patch` from the create-dialogue supervisor into `hydrateRestartableCreateDialogues()` results.
  - [x] Verify the root’s persisted workflow projection still reflects the hydrated machine state after reload.

- [x] Teach resume-input assembly to fall back to normalized `currentMachineStateValue` when `snapshotRef.phase` is absent.
  - [x] Update `buildCreateDialogueResumeInput()` to use the normalized persisted phase source.
  - [x] Add a regression test for migrated records that only contain `currentMachineStateValue`.

- [x] Harden create-dialogue hydration against blank or invalid `flowId` values.
  - [x] Require a trimmed, non-empty `flowId` before phase-specific hydration can complete as supervised.
  - [x] Ensure invalid identifiers fail hydration instead of emitting a malformed supervised result.

- [x] Make hydrated flow-ID alias resolution collision-safe.
  - [x] Preserve all normalized targets for duplicate source IDs instead of overwriting earlier aliases.
  - [x] Add a regression test covering restart hydration with duplicated source flow IDs.

- [x] Rewrite nested flow-bearing fields when canonicalizing hydrated flow IDs.
  - [x] Update `createPlan.flowId` and `pendingCreateDialogueProposals[].flowId` when a record’s flow ID is normalized.
  - [x] Check any other flow-ID-bearing nested payloads for the same restart-time rewrite requirement.

- [x] Rewrite persisted `responseSpec` payloads during flow-id canonicalization so `relatedFlowId` matches the hydrated record.
  - [x] Update restart normalization to rewrite `responseSpec` alongside `createPlan`, pending proposals, and `responseBaseline`.
  - [x] Add a regression test covering a renamed hydrated flow that still needs to attach response drafts.
- [x] Harden `pendingCreateDialogueProposals` hydration against malformed entries.
  - [x] Validate each proposal item before reading `proposal.flowId` or spreading it into normalized output.
  - [x] Add a regression test with a null or undefined proposal entry to confirm hydration skips or rejects it without crashing.
- [x] Canonicalize persisted `responseSpec.relatedFlowId` during restart hydration.
  - [x] Rewrite the stored `responseSpec` object whenever the hydrated canonical flow ID differs or only matches after trimming.
  - [x] Add regression coverage for a renamed or whitespace-padded `relatedFlowId` that must still reattach correctly after restart.
- [x] Harden pending proposal hydration against malformed entries.
  - [x] Validate each proposal item before reading `proposal.flowId` or dereferencing nested `proposedDrafts` fields.
  - [x] Skip or reject malformed items during normalization and add regression coverage for null/undefined and partial proposal objects.
- [ ] Harden pending proposal normalization in both restart and live restart paths.
  - [ ] Validate each proposal object before reading `flowId` or forwarding `proposedDrafts` to authority evaluation.
  - [ ] Reject or skip partial `proposedDrafts` bundles that do not include the required nested draft groups.
  - [ ] Add regression coverage for null/undefined proposal entries and partially shaped `proposedDrafts` objects.
- [ ] Guard rejected-proposal feedback merging against non-iterable values.
  - [ ] Normalize `feedback` to an array before spreading into `mergedFeedback`.
  - [ ] Add regression coverage for truthy non-array `feedback` payloads.
- [ ] Canonicalize `responseSpec.relatedFlowId` during restart hydration.
  - [ ] Rewrite the persisted `responseSpec` when the hydrated canonical flow ID differs or only matches after trimming.
  - [ ] Add regression coverage for stale, renamed, and whitespace-padded `relatedFlowId` values.
## Latest Review Findings

- id: F1
  source: blind-review.md; review-edge-case-hunter.md
  title: Malformed pending proposals can still reach replay and authority checks
  detail: The restart hydrator only verifies that `proposedDrafts` is a non-array object, and the live root path still dereferences `proposal.flowId` before fully validating each pending proposal. As a result, partially shaped records or null/undefined entries can survive normalization and later crash when `evaluateCreateDraftMutationAuthority()` or `compareCoreDraft()` reads required nested draft bundles.
  location:
    - src/stately-studio/actors/shared/restart-hydration.ts:137-152
    - src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts:537-543
    - src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts:867-895
    - src/stately-studio/actors/workflow-reducer.ts:314-321
    - src/stately-studio/actors/shared/create-draft-mutation-policy.ts:82-93

- id: F2
  source: blind-review.md
  title: Malformed `feedback` values can throw during rejected proposal handling
  detail: The hydration guard does not validate `proposal.feedback`. A truthy non-array payload can be retained and later spread into `mergedFeedback` in the rejection branch, where `...(payload.feedback ?? [])` will throw if the value is a plain object or other non-iterable.
  location:
    - src/stately-studio/actors/shared/restart-hydration.ts:137-152
    - src/stately-studio/actors/workflow-reducer.ts:324-328

- id: F3
  source: review-edge-case-hunter.md
  title: Restart hydration preserves stale `responseSpec.relatedFlowId`
  detail: The restart normalizer clones the record and rewrites only a subset of fields, leaving `responseSpec` untouched. If the persisted value is stale, renamed, or whitespace-padded, the runtime can reattach the response spec to the wrong flow after restart.
  location:
    - src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts:524-547

## Prior Review Findings

- id: R-1
  source: blind-review.md; review-edge-case-hunter.md
  title: Canonical flow-id hydration leaves `responseSpec.relatedFlowId` stale
  detail: Restart normalization now canonicalizes persisted `responseSpec` payloads during whitespace-only flow-id matches, so a stored `relatedFlowId` such as `"flow-1 "` is rewritten before response reuse or reattachment.
  location: src/stately-studio/actors/shared/restart-hydration.ts:109-141; tests/stately-studio/restart-hydration.test.ts:214-237

- id: R-2
  source: blind-review.md; review-edge-case-hunter.md
  title: Malformed pending create-dialogue proposals can survive hydration and crash replay
  detail: Restart normalization now rejects malformed pending proposals missing required nested draft bundles, so null/undefined entries and partial proposal objects are skipped before replay handling can dereference them.
  location: src/stately-studio/actors/shared/restart-hydration.ts:126-178; tests/stately-studio/restart-hydration.test.ts:263-294

- Restart hydration only recreates one eligible create flow and can miss additional restartable flows or malformed hydration keys.
- Rehydration payloads drop persisted lifecycle phase, so the recreated child cannot distinguish `dialogue_collecting`, `review_ready`, or `save_pending`.
- Collision-safe hydration still leaves dependent flow references stale when canonical IDs change; `focusedFlowId`, `recentFlowIds`, and nested `responseBaseline.flowId` values must be rewritten alongside the active-flow key.
- Restart hydration loses the create child’s hydrated phase at the supervisor boundary unless the response supervisor carries the persisted phase/current machine state through root restart bookkeeping.
- Root defers child recreation until the next flow-first turn instead of recreating live create dialogue when active workflows are reloaded.
- Restart hydration drops the child supervisor patch unless the root patch includes `focusedFlowId` and `currentTurn.currentMachineStateValue` from the response supervisor output.
- Hydration ignores the normalized `currentMachineStateValue` fallback, causing migrated records without `snapshotRef.phase` to resume as `dialogue_collecting`.
- Phase-specific hydration can succeed with a blank `flowId`, allowing invalid identifiers to be persisted as supervised results.
- Hydrated flow-ID collisions can reopen the wrong record when alias resolution is last-write-wins instead of preserving all normalized targets.
- Collision renaming leaves nested `createPlan.flowId` and `pendingCreateDialogueProposals[].flowId` stale unless they are rewritten with the hydrated key.
- id: EC-1
  source: edge-case-review
  title: Hydrated flow renaming leaves `responseSpec.relatedFlowId` stale
  classification: patch
  detail: Restart normalization rewrites `createPlan.flowId`, `pendingCreateDialogueProposals[].flowId`, and `responseBaseline.flowId`, but it does not rewrite the persisted `responseSpec` payload. Because `prepareResponse()` carries `relatedFlowId` forward and `applyResponseDraftToWorkflowPromptState()` only reattaches drafts when `relatedFlowId` matches the record’s current `flowId`, a renamed hydrated flow can keep emitting drafts tagged with the pre-hydration id and fail to attach to the canonical record.
  location: src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts:515-547; src/stately-studio/actors/shared/response/response-spec-builders.ts:86-127; src/stately-studio/actors/shared/workflow-registry.ts:1170-1175

- id: EC-2
  source: edge-case-review
  title: Malformed `pendingCreateDialogueProposals` entries can crash restart normalization
  classification: patch
  detail: Hydration only guards `record.pendingCreateDialogueProposals` with `Array.isArray(...)` before dereferencing `proposal.flowId` and spreading each entry. If a persisted record contains a null, undefined, or otherwise malformed proposal item, restart hydration can throw instead of skipping the bad entry or failing cleanly.
  location: src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts:537-545
## Dev Notes

### Architecture alignment

- The approved architecture explicitly says that on restart, root reloads active workflow records and, for any non-terminal create flow, recreates `createDialogueMachine(flowId)` from persisted workflow state. [Source: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md` — Shutdown and restart behavior]
- The architecture also says child-local state is derived/disposable and root/workflow record owns durable truth. Rehydration must use persisted workflow artifacts, not child-local snapshots as the sole source of truth. [Source: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md` — Durable vs transient ownership]
- The create-dialogue child’s start/resume input is expected to include workflow artifacts plus `campaignEntityIndex` and `draftRevision`. [Source: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md` — Entity context and lookup support]
- Root is the durable orchestration and policy boundary and owns restart/resume coordination. [Source: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md` — Responsibility boundaries for `chatsendrootmachinev2`]

### Previous story intelligence

- Story 2.1 established the response supervisor vs render split and the existence of a flow-keyed create-dialogue child surface; Story 4.2 should restore that child on restart rather than rebuilding dialogue continuity somewhere else. [Source: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/2-1-split-response-rendering-from-response-supervision.md`]
- Story 2.2 established flow-first ingress into the active create-dialogue child, which makes restart rehydration necessary for conversational continuity after process loss. [Source: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/2-2-route-active-create-turns-through-flow-first-ingress.md`]
- Story 2.3 established that the child should receive read-only `campaignEntityIndex`; Story 4.2 should restore that input from root on restart as part of the same rehydration contract. [Source: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/2-3-provide-campaign-entity-index-to-the-create-dialogue-child.md`]
- Stories 1.1–1.3 established the durable workflow record, stable `flowId`, and baseline/revision surfaces that restart rehydration depends on. [Source: Epic 1 story artifacts]
- Story 4.1 established distinct phases like `review_ready` and `save_pending`; Story 4.2 should restore the correct child behavior under the persisted phase rather than guessing from response text. [Source: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/4-1-separate-review-ready-and-save-pending-phases-under-one-flow.md`]

### Current code reality to build on

- Root already maintains `activeFlows`, `focusedFlowId`, and `recentFlowIds`, making the workflow registry the natural restart source for reconstructing active create flows. [Source: `src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts`; `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/workflow-registry.ts`]
- `resolveActiveWorkflowMatch()` already exists as a workflow-registry helper, which may be useful for active-flow selection, though restart logic will likely need explicit iteration over non-terminal create records rather than only the currently focused flow. [Source: `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/workflow-registry.ts`]
- `responsemachine.ts` still shows a limited top-level input surface in the brownfield code (`responseSpec` only in the currently inspected shape), which is a sign that restart-ready create-dialogue supervisor input assembly remains to be completed. [Source: `src/stately-studio/responsemachine.ts` search results]
- Shared machine protocol helpers already include workflow lifecycle cleanup/shutdown helpers like `clearWorkflowLifecycleOnMatchingComplete`, which is adjacent to but distinct from the restart-time recreation problem. Story 4.2 should focus on recreation, not shutdown. [Source: `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/protocols/machine-setup.ts`]
- `createmachine.ts` still manages `activeFlowId` / `flowStartedAt` / `timeoutAt` and workflow-bound behavior, but restart architecture says create can be recreated lazily when adjudication or persistence is needed. [Source: `src/stately-studio/createmachine.ts`; `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md`]

### Implementation guidance for the dev agent

- Rehydrate from persisted root-owned workflow artifacts, not from response child memory.
- Keep the same `flowId` through restart and child recreation.
- Restore only what is needed for dialogue continuity; avoid eagerly reconstructing more live actors than the architecture requires.
- Use persisted phase metadata to determine how the recreated child should resume.
- If a shared helper is needed to build create-dialogue resume input from a `WorkflowRecord`, place it near workflow/response orchestration code rather than scattering conversion logic inline.

### Suggested file touch list

- `src/stately-studio/chatsendrootmachinev2.ts`
- `src/stately-studio/responsemachine.ts`
- the create-dialogue child machine file introduced in Epic 2
- `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/workflow-registry.ts`
- `src/stately-studio/actors/workflow-reducer.ts` only if typed persisted workflow surfaces need small refinements for restart

### Project Structure Notes

- Keep restart/rehydration work inside `src/stately-studio/` root/response/workflow orchestration modules.
- Do not move restart continuity into UI/session code or generic server modules.
- Preserve the architecture boundary: root reloads and rehydrates, response supervises the recreated child, create remains lazy for adjudication/persistence.

### Testing Requirements

- Add the following tests:
  - one assertion that non-terminal create workflow records can recreate the flow-keyed create-dialogue child on restart
  - one assertion that workflow truth survives loss of child-local memory because root-owned artifacts drive rehydration
  - one assertion that the same `flowId` and persisted phase are preserved after recreation
  - avoid shutdown-specific tests until Story 4.3 lands
- Prioritize restart-path verification and architecture alignment over suite expansion.

### XState / Stately alignment

- Parent/root should own restart-time actor recreation from persisted state.
- Child actors should be reconstructible from explicit typed input.
- Child-local memory should remain disposable.
- State continuity should come from parent-owned persisted context and contracts, not implicit live runtime memory.

- Restart hydration now rewrites persisted `responseSpec` payloads to the hydrated flow ID and skips malformed pending proposal entries during canonicalization.

# Project Context for AI Agents

_This file contains critical rules and patterns that AI agents must follow when implementing code in this project. Focus on unobvious details that agents might otherwise miss._

---

## Technology Stack & Versions

- TypeScript `5.6.3`, Next.js `16`, React `18`, Vitest `4`, XState `5`, Prisma/Postgres, OpenAI SDK `6`.
- `'*`' (see below for file content) path alias is enabled.
- Main live code surfaces:
  - `app/` Next App Router entrypoints and route handlers
  - `components/` UI
  - `lib/` shared runtime/domain helpers
  - `server/` server/runtime integration
  - `src/stately-studio/` rebuilt chat/workflow architecture
- Migration state: rebuilt chat work is in `src/stately-studio`, but some `app/` routes still import `'lib/chat/*`' (see below for file content) seams that do not exist on disk. Do not assume chat migration is complete.

## Critical Implementation Rules

### Language-Specific Rules

- Treat TypeScript contracts as real boundaries; avoid loose casts unless the local file already forces them.
- Follow local import style:
  - `app/`, `components/`, `lib/` usually use `'*`' (see below for file content)
  - deeply nested `src/stately-studio` files usually use nearby relative imports
- Prefer named exports outside framework/config entrypoints.

### Framework-Specific Rules

- Keep Next App Router files aligned with framework conventions and return explicit `NextResponse` values from routes.
- Only use `'use client'` where browser-only hooks are required.
- Preserve existing React/local-state patterns; do not introduce a new state framework.
- For `src/stately-studio`, use the shared protocol files instead of inventing new envelopes:
  - `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/protocols/actor-protocol.ts`
  - `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/protocols/machine-setup.ts`
  - `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/protocols/internal-machine-events.ts`
  - `src/stately-studio/actors/shared/workflow-registry.ts`

### Testing Rules

- Available test commands:
  - `npx vitest run`: default full suite for the live repo surface
  - `npx vitest run tests/stately-studio`: rebuilt chat/workflow tests for `src/stately-studio`
  - `npx vitest run tests/stately`: shared stately/domain helper tests
  - `npx vitest run tests/stately-studio tests/stately`: rebuilt chat architecture plus its shared stately support code
  - `npx vitest run tests/enforcement`: route contracts, policy guards, ref-id rules, retrieval/governance, and app/runtime integration seams
  - `npx vitest run tests/extraction`: extraction, parsing, caching, PDF/text processing, and re-extraction behavior
  - `npx vitest run tests/security`: security-focused tests if/when files exist under that suite
  - `npx vitest run --config vitest.performance.config.ts`: opt-in performance benchmark suite for maybe-disambiguation thresholds
- Pick the narrowest command that matches the files you changed, and use `npx vitest run` when a change crosses multiple app/runtime surfaces.
- Do not recreate deleted legacy `lib/chat` tests; new chat coverage should target the rebuilt `src/stately-studio` architecture.
- `npm run check` is only a partial typecheck; it excludes `app`, `components`, `lib`, `tests`, `scripts`, and `server`.

### Code Quality & Style Rules

- Keep changes surgical and scoped to the approved task.
- Preserve local naming/style within the subsystem you are editing.
- Add comments only for non-obvious intent, invariants, or architectural boundaries.

### Development Workflow Rules

- Start with the smallest read-only investigation that answers the task.
- Report findings before edits when the path is unclear.
- Do not make adjacent refactors or naming cleanups without approval.
- Git safety:
  - never use `git add .`
